AREA 763 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 655, CULTIVATED 8 ; POP 10,922 ( 2010 ), 
20,969 ( 1990 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 6.1 C ( AV 1981-2010 ), PRECIPI 
1,392 MM, SNOW 919 CM ; COUNTRY SIGN = MELON & SKI LIFT.
 
LOCATION  

Yuubari ( yu-u-ba-ri ) ( \u5915\u5F35 ) is a city in Sorachi sub-
prefecture, central Hokkadou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  Yuubari is located in the 
upper reaches of the Yuubari River.  The area is included in the 
Yuubari Mountains.  Forests occupy 86 percent of the area.  #1 

NAME 

The name ``Yuubari'' comes from ``yu-paro'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``hot spring, river mouth.''  Or, it comes from ``i-par'' in Ainu 
language.  It means ``it, mouth.''  Both signify the mouth of the 
Yuubari River.  Yuubari City is located in the upper reaches of the 
river.

In the past, low temperature hot spring ( ``kou-sen'' ) flowed into the 
Yuubari River.  The old Yuubari River ( Kyuu-yuubari River ) joins the 
Chitose River at the border between Naganuma Town, Nanporo Town, and 
Kita-hiroshima City.  The confluence was the mouth of the Yuubari 
River.  #2

HISTORY  

In 1888 ( Meiji 21 ), coal stratum were found along the Shihoro-kabetsu 
River.  ( The Shihoro-kabatetsu River is a tributary of the Yuubari 
River. ) 

In 1892 ( Meii 25 ), Hokkaidou Colliery ( Hokutan ) started coal 
mining.  The Hokkaidou Colliery Railway opened between Yuubari Station 
and Oiwake Station.  ( Oiwake Station is located in Abira Town, Iburi 
sub-prefecture. )

In the same year, the Hokkaidou Colliery Railway also opened the 
sections between Oiwake and Iwamizawa, and between Oiwake and Muroran.
The company started transporting coal from the Yuubari mountains to the 
Port of Muroran, and also to the Port of Otaru via Iwamizawa Station 
and the Horonai Line. 

In 1897 ( Meiji 30 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
was built in Noborikawa ( \u767B\u5DDD ) village.  It was separated 
from Yuni village.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Noborikawa Village ( 2nd-
class municipality ) was established.  #3

In 1918 ( Taishou 7 ), the name was changed from Noborkawa to Yuubari.  
In 1919 ( Taisou 8 ), Yuubari Town ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1943 ( Shouwa 18 ), Yuubari City was established. 

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Nanporo 
  Town. )  
( For stations on Hokkaidou Colliery Railway, see Iwamizawa City in 
  Sorachi sub-prefecture. ) 

CITY HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 
 
The city hall of Yuubari is located along the Shihoro-kabetsu River.  
The location is a narrow valley.  Yuubari Station was in operation near 
the city hall.  When the Sekishou Line opened, the Yuubari Line became 
the Yuubari Branch of the Sekishou Line.  The Yuubari Branch was 
abolished in 2019.  Yuubari Station became Tourist Information Center.

The Sekishou Line and National Route 274 ( Hobetsu-kokudou ) run in the 
south.  Shin-yuubari Station on the Sekishou Line used to be Momiji-
yama Station.  National Route 452 ( Yuubari-kokudou ) branches off from 
National Route 274 near Shin-yuubari Station.  #4 
 
National Route 452 runs along the Yuubari River, passing Lake Shuuparo 
in Yuubari City, Lake Katsurazwa in Mikasa City, and reaches Ashibetsu 
City.  Lake Shuuparo and Lake Katsurazawa are dam lakes.  The national 
highway doesn't lead to the city hall of Yuubari. 

Yuubari was the city of coal mining.  The city flourished during the 
Meiji, the Taishou, and the Shouwa periods.  The population peaked at 
107,972 in 1960 ( Shouwa 35 ) ( by Population Census ).  Today, Yuubari 
is the third least populated city in the country.  #5  #6  #7  #8  #9  
#10

( The city with the smallest population is Utashinai City in Sorachi 
  sub-prefecture, which had 4,390 people in 2010.  The second smallest 
  is Mikasa City in Sorachi sub-prefecture, which had 10,255 people.  
  These were coal mining municipalities. )  
 
( For coal mining, see Akabira City, Ashibetsu City, Bibai City, Kami-
  sunagawa Town, Kuriyama Town, Naie Town, Mikasa City, Numata Town, 
  and Utashinai City in Sorachi sub-prefecture ; Haboro Town, Obira 
  Town, Rumoi City, and Toyotomi Town in Rumoi Sub-prefecture ; Kushiro 
  City and Shiranuka Town in Kushiro Sub-prefecture.  )

( For coal transportation, see Iwamizawa City and Sunagawa City in 
  Sorachi sub-prefecture ; Abira Town and Muroran City in Iburi sub-
  prefecture ; Otaru City in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )
 
INDUSTRY  
 
Distinctive industries in Yuubari are sightseeing and agriculture.  
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = Melons 248 hectares in 2015, pasture 38, 
paddy rice 23, yams 8, fodder 7, tomatoes 5, beans 4, watermelons 1, 
and other vegetables 17 ; dairy cow - ( zero ) in 2015.

As for agriculture, arable land stretches in narrow valleys.  Melon 
cultivation thrives.  The melons are sold under the trademark ``Yuubari 
Melon.''  Recently, medicinal herb cultivation increased.  #11  #12

After the closure of coal mines, Yuubari changed from the city of coal 
mining ( ``tankou'' ) to the city of sightseeing ( ``kankou'' ).  That 
was a policy of Tetsuji Nakada, who served as mayor from 1979 to 2003.

Tourist attractions were established, for example, Coal Museum, Coal 
Historical Village ( Sekitan-no-rekishi-mura ), Melon Castle, Yuubari 
Robot Museum, Mt. Reisui Ski Resort, and Yuubari Film Festival.

Investment in tourism sectors increased, despite the decline in 
population.  The city was designated as a financially restructured 
municipality ( ``zasei-saiken-dantai'' ) in 2007.  Melon Castle, 
the Coal Historical Village, and the Yuubari Robot Museum were closed. 
#13  #14

NOTE  

#1

The upper reaches of the Yuubari River is an incised meander ( ``sen-
nyuu-dakou'' ).  The uplift of the mountains shaped the topography ( by 
the Geospatial Information Authority of Japan ).  

Built-up areas are located in the upper reaches of the Yuubari River.  
Near built-up areas, there are mine-dump mountains.  ( Mine-dump 
mountains or spoil tips are called ``zuri-yama'' in Japanese. )  The 
color of mine-dump mountains is green and black in summer.

The last time I visited the center of Yuubari City was in late April 
2012.  There was still a lot of snow left.  In Sapporo, snow melts in 
early April.
 
( For mine-dump mountain, see Akabira City in Kamikawa sub-
  prefecture. )
( sen-nyuu-dakou = \u7A7F\u5165\u86C7\u884C. )
 
#2

In Ainu culture, the name of river is the name of place.  They lived on 
fish, such as dried salmon.  Salmon swim downstream to upstream before 
winter. 

In Ainu culture, a river begins downstream.  As for the Yuubari River, 
the name ``Yuubari'' comes from Ainu language.  Its downstream areas 
are now Naganuma Town, Kuryama Town, and Yuni Town.  The topography is 
flat.  The Kyuu-yuubari River ( old Yuubari River ) flows through flat 
land.

Yuubari City is located in the mountains.  The lication is upstream of 
the Yuubari River.  The city's former name was Nobori-kawa Village. 
The name ``Nobori-kawa'' means ``climbing, river.''   ( For the name 
``Nobori-kawa,'' see Note No. 3 below. )
 
( For the Yuubari River, see Nanporo Town in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

#3

The name ``Nobori-kawa'' is Japanese.  It means ``climbing, river.''  
It's a translation of ``si-horka-pet'' in Ainu language, which means 
``large, U-turn, river.''  The direction of the Yuubari River's flow 
differs between downstream and midstream.
 
Another interpretation of ``Nobori-kawa'' is that it comes from 
``nupur-pet'' in Ainu language.  The word ``nupur-pet'' means 
``colored, river.''  The colored river may be related to hot springs, 
because the name ``Yuubari'' comes from ``yu-paro'' in Ainu language, 
which means ``hot spring, river mouth.''

The origin of the name Nobori-kawa, meaning ``large, U-turn, river,'' 
is similar to that of Horokanai Town in Kamikawa sub-prefecture. 
Another origin of the name Nobori-kawa, meaning ``colored, river,''
is the same as Noboribetsu City in Iburi sub-prefecture.  
 
#4

Yuubari Station was the terminus of the Yuubari Line ( Yuubari-sen ) 
and the Yuubari Railway ( Yuubari-tetsudou ).  The Yuubari Line 
connected stations of Oiwake, Momiji-yama, and Yuubari.

The Yuubari Line became a branch of the Sekishou Line in 1981.  ( The 
Sekishou Line, which goes through the Yuubari Mountains and the Hidaka 
Mountains, opened in 1981. )  ( In the past, railways detoured the 
Hidaka Mountains.  Travelers used the Nemuro Main Line to cross the 
Hidaka Mountains. ) 

When the Sekishou Line opened, the name Momiji-yama Station was 
changed to Shin-yuubari Station, which means ``New Yuubari Station.''
The name ``Momiji-yama'' is Japanese.  It means ``maple, mountain.''  
Maples are deciduous trees whose leaves turn yellow or red in autumn.

I passed near the former Momiji-yama Station during a bus excursion.  
It was late October.  The mountains were yellow.  I said into the 
microphone, ``The maple trees are very beautiful.''  The pioneers who 
named Momiji-yama may have thought the same way. 
 
As for the former Yuubari Railway ( Yuubari-tetsudou ), it connected 
stations in the municipalities of Yuubari, Kuriayama, Nanporo, and 
Ebetsu.  The railway ran along what are now Prefectural Route 3 and 
Kirara-kaidou. 

The Yuubari Railway was operated by Hokkaidou Colliery.  The Yuubari 
Line was operated by JNR ( Japanese National Railways ), later by JR 
( Japan Railway ).  Trains went through the valleys and arrived at 
Yuubari Station.  The landscape of Yuubari was expressed as ``roadless 
town'' because people used railways.  ( For ``roadless town,'' see Note 
No. 6 below. )
 
#5

The first Population Census in Japan was conducted in 1920 
( Taishou 9 ).  These were the results of Hokkaidou Prefecture at the 
time ;

Hakodate Ward ( 144,000 people ), Otaru Ward ( 108,000 ), Sapporo Ward 
( 102,000 ), Asahikawa Ward ( 61,000 ), Muroran Ward ( 56,000 ), 
Yuubari Town ( 51,000 ), and Kusiro Ward ( 39,000 ).

Today, the population is decreasing in these municipalities except for 
Sapporo.  Yuubari was the town of coal mine.  Yuubari is the third 
least populated municipality in the country. 

( For depopulation, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )
( For Ward ( ``ku'' ) in Hokkaidou, see Kushiro Town in Kushiro sub-
  prefecture. ) 
 
#6

In the newsletter of Hokkaidou Geographical Society published in 1954, 
Kazuo Kameda explained the uniqueness of Yuubari City as follows ( by 
doi.org/10.14917/hgs1951.1954.95 ) ; 

( 1 ) Yuubari is large in area.  ( The built-up areas look narrow 
because they are located in valley bottoms, but the area of the 
municipality is not small. ) 
( 2 ) Male ratio is high.  ( Many bachelor coal miners worked. ) 

( 3 ) Houses use coal stoves in summer.  ( Coal miners could buy coal 
very cheaply.  They used coal stoves even in summer.  Yuubari is a 
mountainous region.  Sometimes it is cold in summer.  Once people get 
used to living in warm rooms, they use heaters even in summer. ) 

( 4 ) 10,000 miners work for Hokutan ( Hokkaidou Colliery & 
Steamship ).  ( It is said that a large coal mine, including Hokutan's 
coal mines, supported 30,000 people.  The situation was called ``hito-
yama, san-man,'' which means ``one coal mine, 30,000 people.'' ) 

( For ``one coal mine, 30,000 people,'' see Bibai City and Mikasa City  
  in Sorachi sub-prefecture. ) 

( 5 ) Beautiful night view.  ( Coal miner's apartments, which were 
called ``tan-juu,'' were packed on slopes.  The night view was like a 
swam of fireflies. )  ( For the night view of coal mine, see Note No.
7 below. )  
 
( 6 ) Busy shopping street on payday.  ( For recreation and shopping in 
coal mining villages, see Iwamizawa City, Kami-sunagawa Town, Snagawa 
City, and Utashinai City in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

( 7 ) Horticulture.  ( Yuubari is mountainous.  Its arable land is 
small.  Horticulture, which utilizes land intensively, develops in such 
areas. )  ( For Yuubari's horticulture, see Note No. 11 below. )
( 8 ) Roadless town. ( People used railways. )  ( For railroads in 
Yuubari, see Note No. 4 above. )  

#7

Coal mine is also expressed as ``light of mountain ( `yamano-
akari' ).''  A mining engineer wrote as follows ; 

``Coal miner's apartments ( `tan-juu' ) were packed on slopes.  The 
night view was like a swarm of fireflies.  My senior coal man said, 
`When you get married, take your wife to Yuubari at night'.'' 

( By Tsuneo Fujita's web-page  http://wp1.fuchu.jp/-fujita3/
  hokutan1-1.htm  Accessed in January 2017.  Archived in HTTP://
  2242.MOOO.COM/HOK/YUUBARI.TXT )

#8

There were 24 coal mines in Yuubari City.  The main six were as 
follows ;
( 1 ) Hokutan Yuubari, operated from 1889 to 1977.  It was located 
north of the city hall. The site is now the Coal Museum.

( The name ``Hokuatan'' is a abbreviation of ``Hokkaidou-tankou- 
  kisen'' in Japanese.  The English name is ``Hokkaidou Colliery & 
  Steamship.''  The old name was ``Hokkaidou Colliery and Railway 
  ( Hokkaidou-tankou-tetsudou ).'' )

In 1913 ( Taishoiu 2 ), Hokutan's branch office moved from Iwamizawa to 
Yuubari.  The headquarters were located in Muroran and Toukyou.  
Hokutan is a Mitsui group company.  Mitsui was one of the huge 
conglomerates called ``zaibatsu'' before the Second World War. 

The guest house of Hokutan is preserved in Yuubari City.  The 
name is ``Hokutan-shikano-tani Club.''  It is located in Shikano-tani 
district near the city hall.  

( The word ``shika'' means ``deer'' in Japanese.  On Japanese playing 
  cards, ``hana-fuda,'' deer and maple leaves are drawn together.  
  ( For maple leaves, see Note No. 4 above. ) ) 

In April 1912, an explosion happened underground, killing 267 miners.  
In December 1912, an explosion happened underground, killing 216 
miners.  In 1920, an explosion happened underground, killing 209 
miners.  In 1965, an explosion happened underground, killing 61 miners.

The novel ``The Crab Cannery Ship ( Kani-kou-sen )'' depicts the 
explosion in 1920.  The novel is known as proletarian literature. ( For 
the novel, see Otaru City in Shribeshi sub-prefecture. ) 

( 2 ) Hokutan Shin-yuubari, operated from 1975 to 1982.  It was located 
in Minami-shimizu-sawa district.  The location was near Shin-yuubari 
Station.  The word ``shin'' means ``new'' in Japanese. 

In 1981, an explosion happened underground, killing 93 miners.  I 
watched the accident on the TV news.  Water was poured into the shaft 
to extinguish the fire.  It was before the miners bodies were pulled 
up.  The site of the shaft is opened, so as air to reach their souls.  
( The shaft is fenced. ) 
 
Hokutan Shin-yuubari was the new coal mine that was supported by 
subsidies from the Japanese government.  New technologies was 
introduced.  High production targets were set. 

( National universities are also supported by subsidies from the 
  government.  I know three professors who died in 15 years at a 
  faculty of a national university. )

( 3 ) Hokutan Heiwa, operated from 1920 to 1930 and from 1937 to 1975.  
It was located in Heiwa district.  The word ``heiwa ( \u5E73\u548C )'' 
means ``peace'' in Japanese.  This coal mine opened after disasters in 
the 1910's and named ``peace'' in the hope of peace.

( 4 ) Hokutan Mayachi, operated from 1905 to 1987.  It was located in 
Mayachi district, near Shin-yuubari Station.  Hokutan Mayachi was the 
last Hokutan's coal mine in Yuubari City.

The last Hokutan's coal mine was in operation in Utashinai City until 
1995.  The name Mayachi ( \u771F\u8C37\u5730 ) comes from ``penke-
maya'' in Ainu language.  ``Penke'' means ``downstream.'' ``Maya'' 
means ``swim, shore.'' 
 
( 5 ) Mitsubishi Ooyuubari, operated from 1929 to 1973.  The site is 
now beneath Lake Shuuparo, which is a dam lake.  Mitsubishi was one of 
the huge conglomerates ``zaibatsu'' before the war, as well as Mitsui
mentioned above. 
 
( 6 ) Mitsubishi Minami-ooyuubari, operated from 1970 to 1990.  The 
site is beneath Lake Shuuparo, as well as Mitsubishi Ooyubari.  It was 
the last underground coal mine in Yuubari City.  ( The last underground 
coal mine in Sorachi sub-prefecture was operated in Utashinai City 
until 1995. ) 

In 1985, an explosion happened underground, killing 62 miners.  As well 
as Hokutan Shin-yuubari, this coal mine was the new one supported by 
subsidies from the government. 

( For taboos among miners, see Utashinai City in Sorachi sub-
  prefecture. )
( For an underground coal mine still in operation, see Kushiro City in 
  Kushiro sub-prefecture. ) 

#9

The coal of Hokkaidou is originated from meta-sequoia deposits.  Meta-
sequoia is a deciduous conifer.  It's a living fossil tree.  It can be 
seen on streets and in parks ( by Nihei lab 2023-2025 ).

The name ``Meta-sequoia'' means ``changed, sequoia.''  Sequoia is the 
world tallest tree, growing in the west coast of the USA, whose height 
reaches 100 meters.  ( ``Sequoia'' is also called ``redwood.'' )  Meta-
sequoia grows at the height of 20 meters. 

During Tertiary period of Cenozoic era, Meta-sequoia grew densely in 
Hokkaidou.  Tertiary period ( obsolete ) was 2.5-65 million years ago.  
The coal of Hokkaidou is new.

In the USA and European countries, coal is mined from older stratum.  
Those were created in the Carboniferous period of Paleozoic era.  
Carboniferous period was 300-350 million years ago. 

In Japanese, Cenozoic era is called ``Shinsei-dai.''  Paleozoic era 
is called ``Kosei-dai.'’  Tertiary period ( obsolete ) is called ``Dai- 
sanki.'' Carboniferous period is called ``Sekitan-ki'' ( by Nihei lab 
2024-2025 ). 

* * *

Coal is used for industrial purposes.  Factories, such as power 
plants, paper mills, and steel mills, use coal and coke.  ( Coke is 
made by heating coal.  Coke furnaces generate high temperatures. ) 

Coal stoves were used until the 1970s in Sapporo City.  I lived in a 
teacher's apartment, which was built in the 1960s.  The building has 
chimneys.  Each room is equipped with a coal deposit.  Pieces of coal 
were found in the deposit and in the apartment's garden. 

From the 1970s, kerosene stoves spread.  From the 2000s, air 
conditioners spread.  Japan is a maritime country.  Today, tankers and 
colliers import oil and coal.  ( There is a huge anchor in a park near 
the Port of Yokohama.  It's a tanker's anchor.  It symbolizes the era 
of oil. )  ( Thinking about an anchor calms me down. )  

#10

A small house that was filmed in ``The Yellow Handkerchief'' ( 1977 
film ) is preserved in Yuubari City.  It's a typical coal miner's 
house.  Coal miner's houses and apartments were the symbol of 
``Ichizan-ikka,'' which means ``one mountain, one family.'' 
 
#11

Yuubari Melons have orange pulp.  The color was unique.  Generally, 
melons have green pulp.  When I traveled to Hokkaidou in the 1990s, I 
stopped at a roadside melon shop.

The seller was a middle-aged woman.  She sold two types of cut melons, 
green one and orange one.  She said, ``Green is sweeter.''  My friend 
said, ``Orange is a cross-bred with pumpkin.''  So, I bought green one. 
The orange one was not Yuubari Melon.
  
As for Yuubari Melon, farmers in Yuubari and staff in agricultural 
experiment station started studying melon cultivation in the 1950s. 
In 1959, the farmers succeeded in obtaining the melon seeds that could 
be used for cross-breeding.  Those were the original seeds.  The 
farmers experimented cross-breeding.

In 1960, a new variety ``Yuubari King'' was born.  In 1961, the first 
melons were sold.  ( Today, melon seeds and seedlings are sold by 
large companies such as Sakata Seed.  It's difficult to obtain the 
original seeds for cross-breeding. )

( Sakata Seed sells famous two melon varieties ; Prince ( bread in 
  1969 ) and Andesu ( bread in 1977 ).  The name ``Prince'' comes from 
  the name of a fruit trading group in Yokohama, not from the Prince 
  Akihito's marrige in 1959.  The name ``Andesu'' comes from Anshin-
  desu in Japanese, not from the Andes Mountains in South America.

  ( continue ) --> The word ``Anshin-desu'' means ``relief.''  It 
  signifies ``relief for cultivation, relief for selling, and relief 
  for eating'' ( by Professor Yuuichi Hashimoto at Hokkaidou 
  University ). ) 
 
Farmers and agricultural cooperatives in Yuubari also adopted a unique 
sales method.  Yuubari Melons were sold through catalogues at 
department stores.  ( We cannot buy Yuubari Melons at supermarkets in 
Honshuu island. )

In 1973, the farmers registered the trademark of Yuubari Melon.  In 
1979, they registered the design of Yuubari Melon sticker, which is 
placed on the melons.  In 1993, they registered the name ``Yuubari 
Melon.''  The registration was difficult, because ``Yuubari'' is a 
common name ( place name ) and ``melon'' is also a common name.
 
I like the font of the trademark.  It reminds me of the wind and 
motorcycles.  ( Yuubari Melon font on the boxes, taken in Yuubari City 
in 1998 --> HTTP://2242.MOOO.COM/POSI/98HOK/6035.JPG ) 

As of 2006, 154 farmers cultivate melons in Yuubari.  The harvest 
amounts 5,000 tons.  Sales total 3,000,000,000 yen.  The peak of the 
harvest is from late June to early August.  Farmers cultivate nothing 
after melons in the year.  They keep honey bees for pollination.

( In the Kantou region, melon farmers cultivate tomatoes after melons. 
  They keep honey bees for melons and bumblebees for tomatoes ( by T. 
  Nihei 2002.  ``Technological innovations in the continuation of 
  greenhouse horticulture in Asahi City.'' http://www.researchgate.net/
  publication/270038030 ). )

( For bumblebee, see Biratori Town in Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

In Yuubari, the first melons are harvested in May.  The melons were 
sown in January and cultivated in hothouses.  The first auction price 
for two Yuubari Melons were 800,000 yen in 2006, 2,000,000 yen in 2007, 
and 1,000,000 yen in 2011.  They were the so-called celebration prices 
( ``go-shuugui-souba'' ), including advertising. 
 
The soil in Yuubari originates from volcanic ash.  Soil temperatures 
rise in summer, both in upland fields and paddy fields.  Moreover, the 
soil has good drainage.  ( Yuubari Melons have been cultivated in paddy 
fields after the enactment of the rice reduction policy ( ``guentan-
seisaku'' ). ) 

Yuubari is a mountainous region.  The temperature drops at night.  So, 
the melons become sweeter.  At cool nights, crops sleep without 
consuming the sugars they produced during the day.
  
#12

As for medicinal herb cultivation, a herbal medicine company, Tsumura, 
cultivates medicinal herbs.  The company also runs a medicinal herb 
processing factory in Yuubari.  These are reasons why the company 
started business in Yuubari ; 

( 1 ) Contract farmers of the company cultivate medicinal herbs in the 
Ishikari Plain and the Tokachi Plain.  Yuubari is located between the 
plains. 
( 2 ) Yuubari is near to the Port of Tomakomai.  Medicinal herbs are s
hipped from the port. 

( 3 ) The company intends to support Yuubari City, where the population 
decreased drastically ( by Nana Ishida 2018.  ``Characteristics of 
medicinal crop production in Hokkaido, Japan'' doi.org/10.7886/
hgs.93.1 ).
  
Medicinal herbs thrive under a cool climate.  For example, Sugadaira 
highland in Nagano Prefecture and Mt. Ibuki in the central Honshuu 
island ( by T. Nihei 2018 ; by Tomohiko Uezu et al. 2007.  
``Characteristic of vegetable production in a high altitude and cool-
climate region.''  http://www.researchgate.net/publication/387948304 ).

#13

I visited the Coal Museum in 2012.  It was during a new-comer's camp 
for Regional Science Course, Faculty of Letters.  The museum was built 
on an old coal mine.  

The museum had exhibitions in old tunnels.  There was a miner's family 
living room.  A supper table was in it.  Dolls of miners were 
displayed.  Dolls of a rescue team were displayed.

I remembered the Yuubari's mining disaster in 1981.  I watched it on TV 
when I was in elementary school.  The Coal Museum was very impressive.
It was scarier than the Haunted Mansion at Disneyland.

excursion organized by the Association of Japanese Geographers.  The 
castle distilled melon brandy.  ( Melon Castle taken in 1998 -->

for my own excursions.  The chief organizer was Professor Katsuhiko 
Yamashita.  The report of the excursion is available from this site ;

 -----------------
AREA 151 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 89, CULTIVATED 26 ; COUNTY = KABATO ; 
POP 4,859 ( 2010 ), 5,537 ( 1990 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 6.9 C ( AV 1981-
2010 ), PRECIPI 1,371 MM ; COUNTRY SIGN = CARNATION & MELON.
 
LOCATION  

Tsukigata ( tsu-ki-ga-ta ) ( \u6708\u5F62 ) is a town in Sorachi sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  Tsukigata is located on 
the west bank of the Ishikari River. 

NAME 

The name ``Tsukigata'' is derived from Kiyoshi Tsukigata, who was the 
first warden of Kabato Prison ( Kabato-shuuchi-kan ).  He was the 
former samurai from the Fukuoka Domain ( now Fukuoka City, Fukuoka 
Prefecture ). 

The name ``Kabato'' comes from ``kapato'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``yellow pond lily,'' which is called ``kouhone'' in Japanese.  Ainu 
people ate the roots of yellow pond lily. 

( Kabato = \u6A3A\u6238. ) 

HISTORY  

In 1881 ( Meiji 14 ), Kabato Prison was established.  The 
administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of Tsukigata village was 
established.  In 1882 ( Meiji 15 ), the county hall ( ``gun-yakusho'' ) 
of Kabato county was established.  It also covered Uryuu county and 
Kamikawa county.  #1  #2

( Today, Uryuu county is located in the north of the Ishikari Plain, 
  Sorachi sub-prefecture.  Kamikawa county is located in the Kamikawa 
  Basin, Kamikawa sub-prefecture. ) 

Those were the first administrative office and county hall in the 
middle and upper reaches of the Ishikari River.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), 
Tsukigata Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.  In 1953 
( Shouwa 28 ), Tsukigata Town was established. 

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Nanporo Town. ) 

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC  

The town hall of Tsukigata is located near the Subetsu River.  National 
Route 275 and Sasshou line pass.  Tsukigata Hot Spring is located near 
the town hall.  #3 

( Subetsu = \u9808\u90E8\u90FD. )
 
INDUSTRY, CULTURE

Distinctive industries in Tsukigata are agriculture and sightseeing.  
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 1,129 hectares in 2015, wheat 
334, beans ( soybeans 292, adzuki beans 4 ), pasture 229, flowers 22 
( greenhouse ), melons 18, buckwheat 17, watermelons 14, tomatoes 10, 

( continue ) --> potatoes 9, fodder 6, radishes 4, and other vegetables 
55+X ( pumpkins ) ; dairy cows X ( concealed ) in 2015.  The area of 
open-field floriculture is concealed.

Floriculture increased after the enactment of the rice reduction policy 
( ``guentan-seisaku'' ) in the 1970s.  Tsukigata became one of the 
largest flower producing municipalities in Hokkaidou.

The area of floriculture in 2005 counted 72 hectares ( open-field and 
greenhouse ), marking the largest in Hokkaidou.  The main flowers are 
lilies, carnations, lisianthus, calla lilies, and delphinums.  
( Lisianthus is called ``Toruko-kikyou'' in Japanese. ) 

As for sightseeing, the old building of Kabato Prison is a tourist 
destination.  It opened in 1973 as the Hokkaidou Prison Museum 
( Hokkaidou- gyoukei-shiryou-kan ).  It reopened in 1996 as the 
Tsukigata-kabato Museum.  It has been designated as Hokkaidou Heritage 
since 2018. 

The novel ``Red People ( Akai-hito )'' by Akira Yoshimura was published 
in 1977.  Stories in the novel are fictional, but they are based on 
real prisoners in Hokkaidou during the Meiji period.  For example, 
Lightning Boy ( Inazuma-kozou ) and 15-centimeter-nail Torakichi 
( Gosun-kugi-no-torakichi ).  #4
 
Shinpachi Nagakura, who had been a swordsman of Shinsen-gumi, 
instructed swordsmanship to the guards of Kabato Prison.  #5 
  
NOTE   
 
#1

Kabato Prison was the first prison in Hokkaidou.  It was a national 
prison in the Meiji period.  Kabato was selected as the location 
because it was easily accessible by boat on the Ishikari River. 

There were two other potential locations for the national prison.  One 
was the Tokachi Plain in Tokachi sub-prefecture.  But, this area was 
remote at the time.  The other was around Mt. Youtei in Shiribeshi sub-
prefecture.  But, this area had already been developed and had many 
houses. 

One reason prisons were built in Hokkaidou was for development. 
Prisoners cleared the wilderness and constructed roads.  Another reason 
was to incarcerate political criminals.  

There were civil wars in the Meiji period, such as the Boshin War, 
Saga Rebellion, Shinpuuren Rebellion, Akizuki Rebellion, Hagui 
Rebellion, and the Satsuma War.  Defeated soldiers became political 
criminals.  

These four prisons were built in Hokkaidou in the Meiji period.
( 1 ) Kabato Prison ( Kabato-shuuchi-kan ) was established in 1881 
( Meiji 14 ), located in what is now Tsukigata Town, Sorachi sub-
prefecture. 

( 2 ) Sorachi Prison ( Sorachi-shuuchi-kan ) was established in 1882 
( Meiji 15 ), located in what is now Mikasa City, Sorachi sub-
prefecture.  

( 3 ) Kushiro Prison ( Kushiro-shuuchi-kan ) was established in 1885 
( Meiji 18 ), located in what is now Shibecha Town, Kushiro sub-
prefecture.  

( 4 ) Abashiri branch of Kushiro Prison was established in 1890 ( Meiji 
23 ), located in what is now Abashiri City, Okhotsk sub-prefecture.

In 1919 ( Taishou 8 ), Kabato Prison was moved to Asahikawa Ward ( now 
Asahikawa City ), Kamikawa sub-prefecture.

Today, prisons in Hokkaidou are located in Sapporo City, Asahikawa 
City, Obihiro City, Kushiro City ( Kushiro branch of Obihiro Prison ), 
Abashiri City, Tsukigata Town, and Hakodate City.  Hakodate is a 
juvenile prison.

The current Tsukigata Prison was established in 1983.  Before that, the 
national government looked for a new location to replace Nakano Prison 
in Toukyou.  Tsukigata Town attracted the new prison and was selected, 
because Tsukigata has a history of the penal system.

Nakano Prison was where the novelist Takiji Kobayashi was imprisoned 
before the Second World War.  He was a proletarian novelist who wrote 
``The Crab Cannery Ship ( Kani-kou-sen ).''  After being released from 
prison, he was tortured to death at a police station.

( For Takiji Kobayashi, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )

I heard that the food of Tsukigata Prison is good.  ( In Hokkaidou, 
food is generally good. )  Prisoners in Tsukigata produce tables and 
chairs, which are called ``ashi-mono ( things with legs ).'' 

Their products are sold at an on-line shipping site of CAPIC 
( Correctional Association Prison Industry Cooperation ).  How about 
buying a wooden chair for your office?  

#2

Prisoners constructed roads in Hokkaidou in the Meiji period.  The 
prisoner-made roads were called ``Shuujin-douro'' in Japanese.  These 
were the major five prisoner-made roads ; 
 
( 1 ) The Kabato Road ( Kabato-douro ) was built from 1886 to 1890 by 
the prisoners of Kabato Prison and Soracih Prison.  The road started at 
Kabato Prison, crossed the Ishikari River by boat, traversed the 
Ishikari Plain, and reached Sorachi Prison. 

The part of the Kabato Road is now Prefectural Route 275.  It connects 
Tsukugata Town and Bibai City, crossing the Ishikari River at 
Tsukigata-oohashi Bridge.  The road can be seen from Mt. Tappu in 
Mikasa City.  ( Sorachi prison was located in Ichikishiri, what is 
now Mikasa City. )

( 2 ) The Kamikawa Road ( Kamikawa-douro ) was built from 1887 to 1889
by the prisoners of Kabato Prison and Sorachi Prison.  The road 
connected Ichikishiri ( now Mikasa City ) and Chuubetsu-buto ( now 
Asahikawa City ).  It's now part of National Route 12. 
 
( 3 ) The Teshio Road ( Teshio-douro ) was built from 1887 to 1892, 
mainly by the prisoners of Kabato Prison.  The road connected Kabato 
Prison ( now Tsukigata Town ) in the Ishikari Plain and Mashike on the 
Sea of Japan side.  It's now part of National Route 275 and part of 
Prefectural Route 94. --> http://cyberjapandata.gsi.go.jp/
#12/43.743817/141.688614 

( 4 ) The Yuubari Road ( Yuubari-douro ) was built from 1889 to 1890, 
mainly by the prisoners of Sorachi Prison.  The road connected 
Iwamizawa, Kakuta ( now Kuriyama Town ), and Yuubari.  It's an old road 
along the foot of the Yuubari Mountains.

( 5 ) The Central Road ( Chuuou-douro ), built in 1891, mainly by the 
prisoners of Abashiri branch of Kushiro Prison.  The road connected 
Asahikawa and Abashiri.  It's now part of National Route 39.
 
The Kabato Road was the first prisoner-made road.  Today, the road 
passes along Miyajima Pond ( Miyajima-numa ) in Bibai City.  The pond 
is the landmark to distinguish the road on the map.

This prisoner-made road is almost straight, but it bends slightly.  
It's like the Hiragana character ``he ( \u3078 )''  --> http://
cyberjapandata.gsi.go.jp/#12/43.301196/141.753502
This is because the road was constructed to divert large ponds.

In the past, logs were laid on the raod.  The logs allowed horse-drawn 
carriages to pass the Ishikari Plain.  Peat-land used to spread on the 
plain.

The logs were cut from Japanese elm trees ( Haru-nire or Akadamo ).  
Japanese elm trees were rampant in the Ishikari Plain in the past.  
Today, the straight road passes through extensive paddy fields. 

( For straight road, see Yuni Town in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

Prisoner-made roads accompanied sacrifice.  The dead were buried along 
the roads.  The burial mounds are called Chain-mounds ( ``kusari-
zuka'' ).  Chain shackles were tombs.  Professor Chiaki Oguchi at 
University of Tsukuba expressed the situation as ``gradual death 
sentence ( `kanman-na-shikei' ).'' 

( For prison and prisoner-made road, see Kuriyama Town, Mikasa City in 
  Sorachi sub-prefecture ; Obihiro City in Tokachi sub-prefecture ;  
  Abashiri City Kitami City, in Okhotsk sub-prefecture ; Shibecha Town, 
  Shiranuka Town, and Teshikaga Town in Kushiro sub-prefecture. )
  
#3

The river name ``Subetsu'' was a place name.  The name``Subetsu'' comes 
from ``si-pet'' in Ainu language.  It means ``large, river.''  The 
etymology is the same as Shibetsu City in Kamikawa sub-prefecture and 
Shibetsu Town in Nemuro sub-prefecture.

The pronunciation ``shi-betsu ( or su-betsu )'' in Japanese sounds like 
``death, separate.''  So, the first Japanese immigrants used the name 
``Kabato'' instead of Subetsu.  Kabato was located in the north.

Today, Kabato-sakai River flows on the border between Urausu Town and 
Shin-totsukawa Town.  The Kabato River also flows in Shin-totsukawa 
Town.  Both municipalities are located north of Tsukigata Town. 

#4

As for the novel ``Red People ( Akai-hito ),'' it depicts prisoners in 
Hokkaidou during the Meiji period.  The prisoners wore red clothes.  
When they escaped, they could be spotted by the red color. So, they 
were called ``red people.''

The novel is fiction, but it's based on real prisoners.  These were 
the prisoners ;

( 1 ) Lightning boy ( Keijirou Sakamoto ) ; a burglar, murderer, 
rapist, and prison escapee.  He was incarcerated in Kabato and other 
prisons.  He escaped from Kabato Prison.  He run away 48-ri ( 188 
kilometers ) per day.  For his speed at running away, he was nicknamed 
``Lightning boy ( Inazuma-kozou ).''

( 2 ) 15-centimeter-nail Torakichi ( Torakichi Nishikawa ) ; an 
attempted murderer, arson ( a'r-son ), and prison escapee.  He was 
incarcerated in prisons of Akita, Kabato, Abashiri, and others.

When he escaped from Akita Prison in Honshuu island, he stepped on a 
board with 15-centimeters' nails ( ``gosun-kugui'' ).  The long nails 
were installed to prevent prisoners from escaping.  So, his nickname 
was 15-centimeter-nail Torakichi ( Gosun-kugi-no-torakichi ).  He was 
also called as the king of jailbreak ( `datsugoku-ou' ).''

( 3 ) Chouan Kumasaka ; a counterfeit banknote maker.  He was a 
mysterious person.  He was a painter, a medical doctor, and a teacher.  
He might have been falsely accused.  He dead in Tsukigata Prison.  His 
life was also depicted in the novel ``Unfortunate Name ( Fuun-na-
namae )'' written by Seichou Matsumoto. 

As for counterfeit banknote maker, the cartoon ``Lupin the Third'' 
depicts one.  Lupin said to the counterfeiter, ``Your work is better 
than the real one.''  Then, the counterfeiter was disappointed. 

( For a story about 15 centimeters' nail, see Hidaka Town in Hidaka 
  sub-prefecture. )
( For Lupin the Third, see Hamanaka Town in Kushiro sub-prefecture. )

#5

Shinpachi Nagakura was the strongest swordsman in Shinsen-gumi.  
Shinsen-gumi was the special forces of the Tokugawa Shogunate in the 
Edo era.  Shinpachi Nagakura's unique sword movement was ``Ryuuhi-ken 
( flying dragon sword ).''  The sword moved from bottom to top.

Shinpachi Nagakura fled the Matsumae Domain ( now Matsumae Town in 
Oshima sub-prefecture ).  After the Meiji period, he lived in Otaru and 
instructed swordsmanship at Kabato Prison and Agricultural College of 
Touhoku Imperial University ( now Hokkaidou University ).

There is a small plaque near the main gate of Hokkaidou University, 
which read ``A place associated with Shinpachi Nagakura.''

( For the swordsman, see Hakodate City in Oshima sub-prefecture. )

|                 |
|      BIBAI      |
|                 |
|  MAP 5 - NO. 8  |
 -----------------
AREA 278 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 123, CULTIVATED 90 ; POP 26,034 
( 2010 ), 35,176 ( 1990 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL TEMP 7.2 C ( AV 1981-2010 ), 
PRECIPI 1,157 MM, SNOW 842 CM ( AV 1982-2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = GOOSE & 
AZALEA.
 
LOCATION  

Bibai ( bi-ba-i ) ( \u7F8E\u5504 ) is a city in Sorachi sub-prefecture, 
central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area of Bibai extends from the 
east bank of the Ishikari River to the Yuubari Mountains.  The area is 
a basin of the Bibai River, which flows from Mt. Bibai ( 987 meters ).  
The western part is mountainous.  The eastern part is rice paddies.

NAME  
 
The name ``Bibai'' comes from ``pipa-o-i'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``mussel ( ma'-sul ), many, place.''  It signified ponds with many 
mussels.  ( ``Mussel'' is ``karasu-gai'' in Japanese. )  There were 
ponds downstream of the Bibai River.  

HISTORY  
 
In 1890 ( Meiji 23 ), Kainuma ( \u6CBC\u8C9D ) village was established. 
The administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) was jointly managed in 
Ichikishiri village ( now Mikasa City ).  The name ``Kai-numa'' means 
``shellfish, pond'' in Japanese.  The shellfish means mussels 
( ``karasu-gai'' ).
 
In 1891 ( Meiji 24 ), Bibai Station on the Hokkaidou Colliery Railway 
( now Hakodate Main Line ) opened.  From 1891 to 1894 ( Meiji 24 to 
27 ), the military settlers' colony ``Bibai-heison'' was established.  
400 families immigrated.

In 1895 ( Meiji 28 ), the administrative office of Kainuma village was 
established in Kainuma village.  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Kainuma Village 
( 2nd-class municipality ) was established.  In 1909 ( Meiji 42 ), 
Kainuma Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was established.  In 1925 
( Taishou 14 ), Kainuma Town was established.  

In 1926 ( Taishou 15 ), the name changed from Kainuma to Bibai.  In 
1950 ( Shouwa 25 ), Bibai City was established.

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Nanporo 
  Town. )

CITY HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 
 
The city hall of Bibai is located where the Bibai River enters the 
Ishikari Plain.  National Route 12 ( Chuuou-kokudou ) and the Hakodate 
Main Line pass.  There is a sign board on National Route 12, which 
reads ``The longest straight road in the country, 29.2 kilometers.'' 

Mitsubishi Bibai Coal Mine and Hokuryou-garo Coal Mine were located in 
the Yuubari Mountains.  Hokuryou-garo was also a Mitsubishi group 
company.  The Bibai Line was operated by Mitsubishi to transport coal.

Mitsui Bibai Coal Mine was located to the south, on the foot of the 
Yuubari Mountains.  ( Mitsui and Mitsubishi were huge conglomerates 
( ``zaibatsu'' ) before the war. ) 
 
The population of Bibai peaked in 1954 at 91,431 ( by Bibai City ).  
Those three coal mines closed in the 1970s.

It was said ``hito-yama, san-man'' in Japanese.  This means that one 
large coal mine ( ``hito-yama'' ) supported 30,000 people ( ``san-
man'' ) ( by Professor Yuuichi Hashimoto at Faculty of Letters, 
Hokkaidou Univresity ). 

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )
( For straight road, see Yuni Town in Sorachi sub-prefecture. ) 
 
INDUSTRY  

Distinctive industries in Bibai are manufacturing, agriculture, coal 
mining, and sightseeing.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 4,351 
hectares in 2015, wheat 2,302, beans ( soybeans 1,174, adzuki beans 8, 
other beans 7 ), buckwheat 180, onions 49, pasture 28, orchards 10 
( other fruits 8+X ), flowers 7 ( greenhouse ), fodder 3, melons 3, 

( continue ) --> strawberries 3, tomatoes 3, carrots 2, cucumbers 2, 
green onions 2, potatoes 2, radishes 2, spinach 2, lettuce 1, other 
vegetables 75+X ( asparagus ), and other grains 42 ; dairy cows - 
( zero ) head in 2015. 

The area of paddy fields is vast.  Rice, wheat, and soybeans are 
cultivated in paddy fields.  Wheat and soybeans increased after the 
enactment of the rice reduction policy ( ``guentan-seisaku'' ).  The 
local specialty is Hasukappu berries, which are processed into jams and 
cakes. 

As for coal mine, an open-pit coal mine continues, although underground 
large-scale coal mines closed.  As for manufacturing, there are two 
industrial parks.  Factories of plastics, metals, semiconductors, and 
food are in operation.  #1  #2

As for sightseeing, Arte Piazza is an art museum dedicated to sculptor 
Kan Yasuda.  It opened in 1992, using an abandoned wooden school 
building.  The building was an elementary school for the children of 
coal miners.  #3

Miyajima Pond on the Ishikari Plain is a stopover point for white-
fronted geese.  ( ``White-fronted goose'' is called ``magan'' in 
Japanese.)  The pond is a Ramsar Convention registered area.  #4

( For Hasukappu berry, see Atsuma Town in Iburi sub-prefecture. )
 
NOTE    

#1

In the past, coal mines were distributed on the map.  Those were 
symbolized by two crossed mattocks and the letter ``coal ( seki-tan, 
\u77F3\u70AD ).''

When I studied geography at junior high school, there were many coal 
mines on the atlas  ( ``chizu-chou'' ).  The atlas is a textbook 
inspected by MEXT ( Ministry of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and 
Technology ).  

Coal mines have disappeared from Japanese atlas long ago.  But, when I 
moved to Hokkaidou and went to Bibai, I found that open-pit coal mines 
were still in operation.  The coal is used in nearby thermal power 
plants.

( For the thermal power plants, see Sunagawa City in Sorachi sub-
  prefecture. ) 
  
#2

Food factories in Bibai produce unique souvenirs, for example, non-melt 
soft cream, fried noodle bags ( ``fukuro-iri yakisoba'' ), Bekanbe-
monaka ( Bekanbe sweet paste of adzuki beans ), and Magan-no-tamago 
( Egg of white-fronted goose ).

The name ``Bekanbe'' comes from ``pe-ka-un-pe'' in Ainu language, which 
means ``water, above, exist, thing.''  That's water caltrops 
( ``hishi'' ).  In an old river ( ``furu-kawa'' ) such as an ox-bow 
lake, water caltrops float. 
 
Magan-no-tamago ( Egg of white-fronted goose ) is a Western-style 
confectionery, whose shape is like a goose egg.  It resembles Kamome-
no-tamago ( Egg of seagull ), which is a souvenir from Iwate 
Prefecture.

Rice flour is a local specialty too.  It's suitable for people with 
wheat flour allergies.  Grilled chicken on skewers ( ``yakitori'' ) is 
also a local specialty.  Especially, grilled chicken offal on skewers 
( ``motsu-kushi'' ) is famous.

Grilled chicken offal on skewers includes chicken liver, inner egg 
( ``kinkan'' ), chicken gizzard ( ``suna-guimo'' ), and chicken heart 
( ``hatsu'' ).  They are inexpensive and nutritious.  They are served 
with a rich sweet sauce.

( For sweet sauce, see Takikawa City in Sorachi sub-prefecture. ) 
( For water caltrop, see Ishikari City in Ishikari sub-prefecture. )
 
As for sweet sauce, those who like chicken eat chicken with salt 
instead of sweet sauce.  With salt, we can eat a lot of chicken.  
Recently, Japanese grilled chicken on skewers became large.  Many 
people eat grilled chicken with salt. 

In Brazil, Brazilians eat a lot of beef with salt.  Brazilian barbecue 
restaurants ( ``churrascarias'' ) also serve grilled chicken heart on 
skewers ( ``espeto de coracao'' ).  Chicken heart is the smallest meat 
in the barbecue restaurants.  It must be a tradition in the food 
culture of manual laborers.
 
#3

As for the art museum Arte Piazza, sculptor Kan Yasuda is from Bibai 
City.  He lives in Pietrasanta, Italy.  His sculptures are made of 
marble.  Pietrasanta is a marble producing site.  

One of Kan Yasuda's sculptures is installed at Sapporo Station.  The 
sculpture is like a big weathered bone.  It's a famous meeting place. 
We say, ``Let's meet at something white at the west exit of Sapporo 
Station.''  ( Its official name is Myoumu. )

#4

Miyajima Pond is located near the Ishikar River.  In the past, there 
were more than 10 ponds near Miyajima Pond.  Many of them were 
reclaimed and turned into rice paddies.  

Flocks of white-fronted geese ( ``magan'' ) come to Miyajima Pond.  The 
arrival season is from late September to early October.  The leaving 
season is from mid April to late April.  White-fronted geese nest in 
Siberia in summer.  They spend in Honshuu island in winter.

( In the past, hunters in Honshuu island shot geese.  A goose was a 
  year-end gift as well as a salmon and a yellow tail. --> http://
  ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%9B%81 )

Miyajima Pond is under the fly way.  Near Miyajima Pond, white-fronted 
geese eat grass seeds, dropped rice ears ( ``ochibo'' ), and second 
ears of rice after harvest ( ``hiko-bae'' ).

|                 |
|     MIKASA      |
|                 |
|  MAP 5 - NO. 9  |
 -----------------
AREA 303 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 247, CULTIVATED 11 ; POP 10,221 ( 2010 ), 
17,049 ( 1990 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = BON DANCE. 
 
LOCATION    

Mikasa ( mi-ka-sa ) ( \u4E09\u7B20 ) is a city in Sorachi sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area extends from 
the Ishikari Plain to the Yuubari Mountains.  It corresponds to the 
upper reaches of the Ikushunbetsu River.  ( The lower reaches are in 
Iwamizawa City. )

Mt. Tappu-yama ( 144 meters ) rises on the edge of the Ishikari Plain.  
This mountain was a landmark for land surveys during the Meiji period. 
The name ``Tappu'' ( \u9054\u5E03 ) comes from ``tap-kop'' in Ainu 
language, which means ``shoulder-like, hump.''  It signifies a round 
hill.  #1 

NAME 

The name ``Mikasa'' comes from Mt. Mikasa-yama ( 594 meters ).  In the 
Meiji period, a prisoner in Sorachi Prison named the mountain ``Mikasa-
yama'' after a mountain in his hometown in Nara Prefecture.  #2

HISTORY 

In 1879 ( Meiji 12 ), Horonai Coal Mine opened.  In 1882 ( Meiji 15 ), 
Sorachi Prison ( Sorachi-shuuchi-kan ) was established.  The Horonai 
Line opened for coal transportation.  Prisoners worked in the coal 
mine.  #3

In 1884 ( Meiji 17 ), the administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) 
of Ichikishiri and another village ( Horonai ) was established.  In 
1889 ( Meiji 22 ), the administrative office of Ichikishiri and two 
other municipalities ( Horonai village and Yuubari county ) was 
established. 

In 1891 ( Meiji 24 ), the administrative office of Ichikishiri and four 
other municipalities ( Horonai, Ikushunbetsu, Kainuma villages, and 
Yuubari county ) was established.  In the same year, Yuubari county was 
separated and included in Sapporo and nine counties' administrative 
office ( ``gun-yakusho'' ).

In 1895 ( Meiji 28 ), Kainuma village was separated.  ( Kainuma village 
was what is now Bibai City. )  In 1906 ( Meiji 39 ), Mikasa-yama 
Village ( 2nd-class municipality ) was established by the merger of 
Ichikishiri, Horonai, and Ikushunbetsu villages.

In 1923 ( Taishou 12 ), Mikasa Village ( 1st-class municipality ) was 
established.  In 1942 ( Shouwa 17 ), Mikasa Town was established.  In 
1957 ( Shouwa 32 ), Mikasa City was established. 

( For 1st-class and 2nd-class municipalities, see the note of Nanporo 
  Town. )
( For the Horonai Line, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )

( Ichikishiri = \u5E02\u6765\u77E5.  Horonai = \u5E4C\u5185. 
  Ikushunbetsu = \u5E7E\u6625\u5225.  Mikasa-yama = 
  \u4E09\u7B20\u5C71. )

CITY HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 

The city hall of Mikasa is located near the confluence of the Horonai 
River and the Ikushunbetsu River.  National Route 12 ( Chuuou-kokudou ) 
runs to the west.  In the past, the Horonai Line ran.

Horonai Coal Mine was the first modern coal mine in the country.  It 
was run by Hokutan ( Hokkaidou Colliery & Steamship ).  Also, Ponbetsu 
Coal Mine was in operation.  It was run by Sumitomo, which was one of 
the large conglomerates ``zaibatsu'' before World War II.

Sumitomo's Ponbetsu Coal Mine was closed in 1971.  Hokutan's Horonai 
Coal Mine was closed in 1989.  The population of Mikasa peaked at 
62,781 in 1959 ( by Mikasa City ).

It was said, ``one mountain, thirty thousand ( `hito-yama, san-
man' ).''  This means that a large coal mine supported 30,000 people.  
Today, Mikasa is the second least populated city in the country.
( The least populated city is Utashinai in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

INDUSTRY  

Distinctive industries in Mikasa are manufacturing, agriculture, and 
sightseeing.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 227 hectares in 
2015, onions 203, wheat 117, pasture 67, fodder 65, soybeans 62, 
orchards 35 ( grapes 20 ), buckwheat 25, cabbage 12, melons 10, 

( continue ) --> watermelons 7, Chinese cabbage 6, cucumbers 5, 
radishes 1, tomatoes 1, and other vegetables 29+X ; dairy cows X 
( concealed ) head in 2015. 

As for manufacturing, Mikasa Industrial Park was established after coal 
mines closed.  The industiral park is located on the edge of the 
Ishikari Plain.  There are factories of agricultural machinery, food 
processing, and construction. 

A large shopping mall run by AEON is also located in the industrial 
park.  To attract the shopping center, the land use category was 
changed from industrial to commercial.  

As for agriculture, Mikasa melon is a local specialty.  There is a 
vineyard run by Yamazaki Winery on the slope of Mt. Tappu.  AEON runs a 
farm in the city to sell local vegetables in the shopping mall.  #4

Sightseeing sites include the Mikasa Railroad Memorial Museum, the 
Mikasa City Museum, Mikasa Geopark, and Lake Katsurazawa.  Around 
Mikasa Railroad Memorial Hall, coal miner's apartments ( ``tan-juu'' ) 
spread.  The apartments are not in use.  They are the former site of 
Hokutan coal mine.  #5  #6  #7
 
At for the former Sumitomo Ponbetsu Coal Mine, a shaft tower ( ``tate-
kou'' ) remains.  It creates a unique landscape.  On a bus excursion of 
the Hokkaidou Geographical Society in 2010, we could approach the shaft 
tower.  A city hall employee guided us. 

NOTE   

#1

Regarding Mt. Tappu, we can watch the Ishikari Plain from an 
observation deck at the top of the mountain.  Mt. Tappu was a popular 
sightseeing spot in the Meiji period.  The rest area was crowded.  
People in the past also watched the Ishikari Plain from the mountain.

( A proverb says, ``Smoke and fools go to high places.'' ( Fools are 
  easily flattered. )  My teachers said ``Smoke and geographers go to 
  high places.'' )  

#2

The original Mt. Mikasa-yama is located in the fringe of the Nara 
Basin, Nara Prefecture.  A prisoner from the Nara Basin called the 
mountain near the prison ``Mt. Mikasa.''  It's a thinking-of-hometown 
mountain ( ``bou-kyou-yama'' ( \u671B\u90F7\u5C71 ) ).  Japan is a 
mountainous country.  

#3

The first railroad in the country opened in 1872 ( Meiji 5 ).  It 
connected the Port of Yokohama and Shinbashi in Toukyou.  The second 
railroad opened in 1874 ( Meiji 7 ).  It connected the Port of Koube 
and Oosaka.  The third railroad was the Horonai Line in Hokkaidou. 

The Horonai Line connected the Port of Otaru and Sapporo in 
1880 ( Meiji 13 ).  The railroad was extended to Horonai in 1882 
( Meiji 15 ) and started coal transportation.  The first three 
railroads were operated by the Meiji government. 
 
In 1889 ( Meiji 22 ), the management of the Horonai Line was 
transferred to the Hokkaidou Colliery Railway ( Hokkaidou-tankou-
tetsudou ).  Later, the company's name was changed to Hokkaido Colliery 
& Steamship ( Hokkaidou-tankou-kisen ).  The company is commonly known 
as Hokutan.  

Horace Capron was an American advisor, who worked for the Hokkaidou 
Development Commission ( Kaitakushi ) from 1871 to 1875.  He suggested 
transporting coal to the Port of Muroran on the Pacific coast.

Horace Capron was invited by Kiyotaka Kuroda.  Kiyotaka Kuroda was the 
undersecretary ( vice minister ) of the Hokkaidou Development 
Commission.  He was an officer of the new Meiji government forces 
during the Boshin War. 

( For Kiyotaka Kuroda, see Hakodate City in Oshima sub-prefecture. ) 

Benjamin Smith Lyman was an American geologist and engineer.  He worked 
for the Hokkaidou Development Commission from 1872 to 1876 and for the 
Japanese government from 1876 to 1879.  He conducted geological surveys 
in Hokkaidou, discovered coal seams in Horonai, and suggested the 
possibility of the coal mining industry. 

Benjamin Smith Lyman also suggested transporting coal to the Port of 
Otaru on the Sea of Japan side.  The Hokkaidou Development Commission 
adopted this plan, instead of Horace Capron's Muroran plan, because it 
was less expensive. 

Joseph Ury Crawford was an American railroad engineer.  He worked for 
the Hokkaidou Development Commission from 1878 to 1881.  He supervised 
the construction of the Horonai Line.  The former Mikasa Station is now 
Crawford Park.

( For the Horonai Line, see Otaru City in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )

#4

Yamazaki Winery is a family-run winery.  They don't produce in 
quantities.  Their wines are hard to find, but sold at a local 
supermarket.  It's A-coop ( agricultural cooperative market ).

( For wine, see Ikeda Town in Tokachi sub-prefecture. )
 
#5

The Mikasa Railroad Memorial Museum is located on the former site of 
Horonai Station.  It was the terminus of the Horonai Line.  ( The 
Horonai Line was the third railroad in the country. )  The museum 
maintains a steam locomotive that can move.

( The other terminus of the Horonai Line was Temiya Station in Otaru 
  City.  The former site of Temiya Station was the Otaru Railway Museum 
  ( now Otaru Museum ). )
  
The landscape of coal miner's apartments ( ``tan-juu'' ) is withered.  
When I saw them, I wanted to live there for about two years.  I would 
think deeply during the long winter. 
 
Coal miners bought coal very cheaply.  They lit stoves even in summer.  
Houses in Hokkaidou are warm.  Even in winter, the rooms are like 
summer.  Once people get used to it, they use stoves in summer. 

In Chuuou Ward, Sapporo City, ice cream vendors come to residential 
areas during the winter.  People eat ice cream and drink cold beer in 
hot rooms.

But, houses in Hokkaidou used to be very cold.  An elderly man said, 
``On winter mornings, the moisture from my nose froze my bedding 
cover.''  In the pioneering years, babies died on cold nights.

It's still cold in the early summer in Hokkaidou.  It's like Canada. 
In Sapporo City, athletic festivals at elementary schools are held in 
early summer.  I remember that an athletic festival held at Sankaku-
yama elementary school in Sapporo City.  The date was 3rd June, 2017.

At the time, the temperature was 10 degree Celsius.  ( It was 
measured in the central are of Sapporo.  Sankaku-yama elementary school 
is located on the foot of Mt. Sankaku-yama, so the temperature was 
cooler than this. )  Teachers prepared a heating room for parents who 
came to see the athletic festival. 

( For athletic festival, see the note of Hidaka Town in Hidaka sub-
  prefecture. )

#6

Cretaceous period strata exist in Mikasa City.  ``Cretaceous'' is 
called ``Hakuaki'' in Japanese.  It's a geological period from 65 to 
150 million years ago.  Ammonite fossiles have been excavated from the 
Cretaceous strata.

The Mikasa City Museum exhibits fossiles of ammonites and Ezo-mikasa-
ryuu ( ``Taniwhasaurus'' ).  The ammonite fossils are larege.  Ezo-
mikasa-ryuu is a dinosaur fossil.  It has been designated as a natural 
monument. 

Elementary school students in Sapporo City have visited the Mikasa City 
Museum as part of their school trip.  Geo-tours are also popular 
recently.  Geo-tours will be canceled if a bear is spotted.

Mikasa City is a mecca for young fossil enthusiasts.  One of the 
curators of the museum is from Aichi Prefecture.  ( Aichi Prefecture is 
located in central Japan .)  When he was in the sixth grade of 
elementary school, he tried to visit Mikasa City by bicycle.  His 
parents stopped him.  
  
#7

Lake Katsurazawa is located in the eastern part of Mikasa City.  The 
lake was depicted on the previous country sign ( gateway sign ).  The 
area around the lake is a fossil excavation area.  Ammonites and the 
dinosaur Ezo-mikasa-ryuu were unearthed there.  The dinosaur was
unearthed in 1976.  The Mikasa City Museum opened in 1979.
 
Lake Katsurazawa is an artificial lake.  The Katsurazawa Dam is one of 
the oldest dams in Hokkaidou.  It was constructed between 1947 and 
1957.  The dam site is part of Furano Ashibetsu Natural Park.  ( The 
Natural Park ( Shizen-kouen ) is designated by Hokkaidou government. )
Katsurazawa Hot Spring is located downstream from the lake.

|                  |
|      URAUSU      |
|                  |
|  MAP 5 - NO. 10  |
 ------------------
AREA 101 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 49, CULTIVATED 33 ; COUNTY = KABATO ; POP 
2,206 ( 2010 ), 3,058 ( 1990 ) ; MEAN ANNUAL PRECIPI 1,480 MM ( AV 
1983-2010 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = GRAPE & CHERRY.  
  
LOCATION   

Urausu ( u-ra-u-su ) ( \u6D66\u81FC ) is a town in Sorachi sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area is located on 
the west bank ( right bank ) of the Ishikari River.  Urausu is an 
inland town.  The location is in the middle reaches of the Ishikari 
River.  #1 

Mt. Urausu ( 718 meters ) stands near the center of the town.  Oxbow 
lakes remain in the Ishikari Plain.  ( Oxbow lakes are called 
``mikazuki-ko'' in Japanese, which means ``crescent moon lakes.'' ) 

NAME  

The name ``Urausu'' is derived from ``uray-us-pet'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``fish trap, many, river.''  It signified the Urausu River.  
Ainu people put fish trap ( ``yana'' ) in the river to catch salmon.  
They lived on dried fish during the winter.

HISTORY 

In 1887 ( Meiji 20 ), prisoners from Kabato Prison constructed a road 
between Tsukigata and Okisonai ( \u6669\u751F\u5185 ).  Then, Japanese 
people immigrated.  ( Okisonai is located in the southern part of 
Urausu Town.  Kabato Prison was located in Tsukigata Town. )

In 1899 ( Meiji 32 ), an administrative office ( ``kochou-yakuba'' ) of 
Urausu village was established.  It was separated from Tsukigata 
village.  In 1909 ( Meiji 42 ), Urausu Village ( 2nd-class 
municipality ) was established.  In 1960 ( Shouwa 35 ), Urausu Town 
was established.  #2

( For 2nd-class municipality, see the note of Nanporo Town. )

TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC

The town hall of Urausu is located near the Urausu River.  National 
Route 275 ( Sorachi-kokudou ) and the Sasshou Line pass.

NDUSTRY 

Distinctive industry in Urausu is agriculture, including viticulture.  
STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 1,610 hectares in 2015, wheat 
315, pasture 273, buckwheat 193, orchard 131 ( grapes X 
( concealed ) ), beans ( soybeans 106, adzuki beans 6 ), fodder 31, 

( continue ) --> potatoes 16, melons 13, flowers 4, tomatoes 4, 
watermelons 1, other vegetables 32+X, and other grains 12 ; dairy cows 
X head in 2015, and beef cattle X.

As for viticulture, Tsurunuma vineyard is located on the foot of Mt. 
Urausu.  The vineyard covers 450 hectares.  Although the area is 
concealed in the census, it's the largest vineyard in the country.  The 
landscape is like a foreign country.

The vineyard is cultivated by Hokkaidou Wine in Otaru City, Shiribeshi 
sub-prefecture.  There is a wine shop in the vineyard.  The name is 
Tsurunuma winery.  ( The brewery is located in Otaru City. )  #3 

( ``Tsuru-numa'' is a place name, which means ``crane, pond'' in 
  Japanese.  In the Meiji period, the Ministry of the Imperial 
  Household ( Kunai-shou ) designated the area as a crane breeding 
  area. )
 
Other local specialties include buckwheat, asparagus, and beef cattle. 
The buckwheat is an old variety called Botan-soba.  The cattle is a 
brown breed called Akague-washu.  #4  #5

NOTE  
 
#1

From the Hokkadou Expressway ( Dou-ou-dou ), Mt. Pin-ne-shiri stands 
out.  The altitude is 1,100 meters.  The mountaintop still has snow at 
the end of May.

The summit of Mt. Pin-ne-shiri is the border between Shin-totsukawa 
Town and Toubetsu Town.  The summit is not included in Urausu Town. 
The name ``Pin-ne-shiri'' comes from ``pin-ne-shir'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``man ( male ), be, mountain.''

Mt. Kumaneshiri ( \u9688\u6839\u5C3B ) stands near Mr. Pin-ne-shiri.  
The altitude is 971 meters.  Mt. Kumaneshiri is the border between 
Urausu Town, Tsukigata Town, and Toubetsu Town.

The name ``Kumanehsiri'' comes from ``Kuma-ne-sir'' in Ainu language, 
which means ``fish-drying-shelf, be, mountain.''  It signified the flat 
mountaintop that looks like a fish drying shelf.

( For Mt. Pin-ne-shiri, see Shin-totsukawa Town in Sorachi sub-
  prefecture and Kimobetsu Town in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. )
 
#2

In 1893 ( Meiji 26 ), Takechi Ansai ( 1847-1894 ) built a Protestant 
church in Urausu.  ( At the time, Urausu was included in Tsukigata 
village. )

He resigned from his seat in the House of Representatives ( Shuu-gui-
in-gui-in ) and engaged in the development of Urausu.  He died of a 
cerebral hemorrhage at the age of 47.  At the time, he was on board the 
Seikan Train Ferry.  He was returning to his hometown in Kouchi 
Prefecture.  

Then, Naohiro Sakamoto ( 1953-1911 ) supported the church and land 
development in Urausu.  He also led a Protestant group and its land 
development in Kunnepu ( now Kitami City ), Okhotsk sub-prefecture.

He was a nephew of Ryouma Sakamoto from Tosa country ( now Kouchi 
Prefecture ).  Ryouma Sakamoto was an activist who sought to end the 
Tokugawa Shogunate ( by T. Nihei 2018 ).   
  
#3

Rice was the main product of Urausu.  In the 1970s, paddy fields 
expanded in the country, and people ate more Western food.  Rice 
production exceeded demand.  Around the time, the mayor of Urausu Town 
asked the founder of Hokkaidou Wine to make a vineyard.  In 1972, land 
clearing started. 
 
The climate of Urausu is similar to Germany.  So, the engineers of 
Hokkaidou Wine aimed to make German-style wine.  They imported grape 
seedlings from Germany in 1973.  The seedlings were preserved in 
quarantine for one year.  They were planted in 1974.  They died by 
withering or being eaten by wild animals.  

In 1977, Hokkaidou Wine employed a Kellermiester ( wine-cellar master ) 
from Germany.  By the Kellermiester's techniques, the vineyard expanded 
on the slope of Mt. Urausu.

( As for wine, I visited wineries in Canada.  At a small wine shop, a 
  man said, ``Keeping clean glasses is important.''  Those were 
  impressive words.  ( For Canadian wineries, see T. Kikuchi et 
  al. 2016.  Development of wineries and its impact on rural 
  commodification of Cowichan Valley in Vancouver Island.  ``Chiri-
  kuukan'' http://www.researchgate.net/publication/342436377 )

( For wine, see Ikeda Town in Tokachi sub-prefecture. ) 

#4

Botan-soba is an old buckwheat variety.  The word ``botan'' means peony 
flower.  Peony was the king of flowers in old China.  It is said that 
the buckwheat variety was bred in Hokkaidou around 1930.  The origin of 
the variety may be Botan-daira district in Kuroishi City, Aomori 
Prefecture.
 
The fragrance of Botan-soba is excellent, but it is difficult to 
cultivate.  Today, the main buckwheat variety in Hokkaidou is ``Kita-
wase.''  Kita-wase has a weak fragrance, but the yield is high.

( For buckwheat, see Horokanai Town in Kamiakwa sub-prefecture. )

#5

Jinnai Farm 21 is located near Tsurunuma winery.  The farm runs 
cottages with vegetable gardens.  It also grazes Japanese brown cattle, 
which are called Akague-washu.

Brown cattle have more lean meat and less fat than Japanese black 
cattle, which are called Kurogue-wagyuu.  Japanese brown cattle 
( Akague-washu ) are grazed mainly in Kyuushuu and Shikoku islands.  
In Hokkaidou, brown cattle are generally Japanese short-horn, 
which are called Nihon-tankaku.

In the past, Jinnai Farm 21 cultivated mangoes in a greenhouse.  It was 
the northern limit for mango cultivation in the country.  The founder 
of the farm was Ryouichi Jinnai.  He was also the founder of Promise, 
which is a legal moneylender company ( ``sara-kin'' ).  He was born and 
raised on a farm in Shikoku island. 

( For Japanese short-horn cattle, see Hokkaidou University Ranch in 
  Shin-hidaka Town, Hidaka sub-prefecture. )

( Akague-washu = \u8910\u6BDB\u548C\u7A2E.  Kurogue-wagyuu = 
  \u9ED2\u6BDB\u548C\u725B.  Nihon-tankaku = 
  \u65E5\u672C\u77ED\u89D2. )

|                  |
|       NAIE       |
|                  |
|  MAP 5 - NO. 11  |
 ------------------
AREA 88 KM2 ( 2010 ), FOREST 49, CULTIVATED 20 ; COUNTY = SORACHI ; 
POP 6,194 ( 2010 ), 8,075 ( 1990 ) ; COUNTRY SIGN = MELON & NANA-
KAMADO.  #1 
  
LOCATION    

Naie ( na-i-e ) ( \u5948\u4E95\u6C5F ) is a town in Sorachi sub-
prefecture, central Hokkaidou ( ``dou-ou'' ).  The area extends from 
the east bank ( left bank ) of the Ishikari River to the Yuubari 
Mountains.  Naie is a small inland town. 

NAME 

The name ``Naie'' comes from ``naey'' in Ainu language.  It means 
``valley river.''  Or, the name comes from ``naye'' in Ainu language.  
It means ``the river.''  It signified the Naie River.  

HISTORY 

In 1891 ( Meiji 24 ), Naie Station on the Hokkaidou Colliery Railway 
( now Hakodate Main Line ) opened.  In 1944 ( Shouwa 19 ), Naie Village 
( 1st-class municipality ) was established.  It was separated from 
Sunagawa Town.  In 1950 ( Shouwa 25 ), Naie Town was established.  
( Cf. Sunagawa was called ``Nae'' from 1890 to 1903. )  #2

( For 1st-class municipality, see the note of Nanporo Town. ) 
 
TOWN HALL, TRAFFIC, POPULATION 

The town hall of Naie is located near the Naie River.  The built-up 
area was developed on the terrace of the Ishikari River.  National 
Route 12 ( Chuuou-kokudou ) and the Hakodate Main Line pass.

The national highway is the longest straight road in the country.  Its 
straight part continues 29 kilometers long.  Naie is at the halfway 
point.  ( As for the word ``halfway,'' the President of the USA, 
Theodore Roosevelt, said, ``Believe you can and you're halfway 
there.'' )

The population peaked in 1960 at 18,458 ( by Population Census ).  In 
the past, there were Sumitomo Naie Coal Mine and Mitsui Oku-naie Coal 
Mine.  Mitsui Oku-naie closed in 1967.  Sumitomo Naie closed in 1971. 
#3 

( For coal mining, see Yuubari City in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )

INDUSTRY   

Distinctive industries in Naie are manufacturing, agriculture, and 
power generation.  STATISTICS OF AGRICULTURE = paddy rice 1,233 
hectares in 2015, wheat 180, beans ( soybeans 55, adzuki beans 3 ), 

( continue ) --> buckwheat 49, pasture 48, fodder 45, tomatoes 20, 
broccoli 3, flowers 3, cabbage 1, cucumbers 1, other vegetables 6+X, 
other grains 17, and melons X ; dairy cows 256 head in 2015.

Local specialties are melons and lily bulbs.  Melon cultivation in 
Hokkaidou started in Yuubari City, Naie Town, Mikasa City, and Hobetsu 
Town ( now Mukawa Town ).  Amoung the regions, Yuubari, Naie, and 
Mikasa are the former coal mining municipalities.  Hobetsu is a 
mountainous region. 

( For lily bulb, see Makkari Town in Shiribeshi sub-prefecture. ) 

As for manufacturing, Kamaya Electric Co. produces electronic 
components.  Hokkaidou Sumiden Seimitsu, a Sumitomo Group company, 
produces cemented carbide ( ``choukou-goukin'' ).  Hokkaidou Denki, a 
Sumitomo Group company, produces electric wires.

After coal mines closed, Naie town established a municipal industrial 
park and attracted those factories.  As for sightseeing, there is Naie 
Hot Spring.  It was built at the former site of Mitsui Naie Junior High 
School, which closed in 1971.  The town runs the hot spring.

I stayed the hot spring during a training camp for new faculty members 
at Hokkaidou University.  As for landscape, the Hokkai Main Canal 
( Hokkai-kansen-yousuiro ) flows near Naie Sunagawa Interchange on the 
Hokkaidou Expressway.  The canal is for agricultural irrigation.  The 
waterway meanders along the natural terrain.  It's a beautiful canal.  
#4  #5  #6 

NOTE       

#1

Nana-kamado is called rowan in English.  It is also known as mountain-
ash.  In Honshuu island, Nana-kamado grows on high mountains.  I saw 
Nana-kamado trees on Mt. Neko-dake ( 2,207 meters ) during an excursion 
by the Department of Tourism Science, Toukyou Metropolitan University.
 
In Canada, Nana-kamado trees grow suburban lowlands.  It Hokkadou 
island, Nana-kamado trees also grows from zero meters above sea level.  
Nana-kamado trees are beautiful.  They are planted along avenues in 
cold climate regions.

The name ``Nana-kamado'' means ``seven, kitchen-stove.''  It signifies 
that the wood is so hard that it can be burned seven times in a kitchen 
stove.  ( I think Japanese ash ( ``Aodamo'' ) is harder than Nana-
kamado. )

Nana-kamado trees bear red berries throughout the winter.  In December, 
birds don't eat the berries.  In late February, birds eat them.  Maybe, 
birds are very hungry at the time.  Or, the berries become delicious.  
( In Europe, people make jam from berries of rowan trees. )

#2

Around the year Naie Station opened, the Colonial Rectangular Land 
Division ( Shokuminchi-kukaku ) was completed in Nae-guen-ya.  The name 
``Nae-guen-ya'' means ``Nae wilderness.''  It was the second Colonial 
Rectangular Land Division.  Nae-guen-ya district remains in Nae Town.  

( For the first Colonial Rectangular Land Division, see Shin-totsukawa 
  Town in Sorachi sub-prefecture. )
( Nae-guen-ya = \u5948\u6C5F\u539F\u91CE. )
 
#3

At the mid-point of the longest straight road in the country, there is 
Hausu-yarubi Roadside Station.  The name comes from the sister city 
Hausjarvi ( Hausj\u00E4rvi ) in Finland. 

In 1994, Naie Town sent a research team to Hausjarvi, including 
assembly members, doctors, and welfare officials.  They intended to 
make a welfare town after coal mines closed.

( Japanese people think that the people in north Europe lead happy 
  lives.  That may be a delusion.  There is a positive correlation 
  between happiness and drug use.  ( Happiness is self-esteem and the 
  drug is antidepressant. )  In the past, suicide rates were higher in 
  regions of cloudy and cold climates. )  

#4

A training camp for Hokkaidou University's new faculty members was held 
in Naie Town in 2010.  It was fun.  At night, a party continue 
endlessly.  An assistant professor from Dental School said, ``during 
the campus festival, teachers pay 10,000 yen for a pack of `takoyaki' 
( octopus dumpling ) that students bring to their offices.''

An associate professor from Faculty of Letters ( me ) said, ``I ate 
150-yen instant `yakisoba' for lunch.''  ( Takoyaki and ``yakisoba'' 
( sauce noodle ) are sold at campus festivals. )

As for instant ``yakisoba,'' Yakisoba-bentou ( Yakisoba lunch box ) of 
Seicomart comes with powder for making soup.  The hot water for making 
``yakisoba'' can be reused.  ( Seicomart is a convenience store chain 
in Hokkaidou. )  
  
#5

The headwater of the Hokkai Main Canal ( Hokkai-kansen-yousuiro ) is 
located in Akabira City, Sorachi sub-prefecture.  The water comes from 
the Sorachi River.

The headwaters of canals are called ``toushu-kou ( \u982D\u9996
\u5DE5 )'' in Japanese.  It means ``head, neck, engineering.'' 
That's a difficult but interesting word.  God creates light at first.  
Engineers create headwaters at first.

The Hokkai Main Canal ends in Nanporo Town, Sorachi sub-prefecture.  
The length is 80 kilometers.  It was the longest irrigation canal 
before World War II.  ( Currently, the longest irrigation canal is the 
Toyogawa Canal in Aichi Prefecture, whose length is 98 kilometers. )

The elevation difference of the Hokkai Main Canal is merely 28 meters. 
Siphons and aqueducts are used.  The waterway meanders.  The canal 
construction project started in 1922.  The canal was constructed from 
1924 to 1929.  There was no heavy machinery at the time.  
 
Enginners Naka Tomonari ( 1857-1931 ) and Eiji Hiraga ( 1892-1982 ) led 
the project.  ( ``Tomonari'' is a family name. )  In winter, they hired 
horse-drawn sleighs and transported stones on the frozen Sorachi River. 
In spring, they began constructing the headwater.

The stones were cubic stones that were extracted and processed 
in Noboribetsu, Iburi sub-prefecture.  Cubic stones for construction 
are called ``kenchi-ishi ( \u9593\u77E5\u77F3 )'' in Japanese.

( One cubic stone is 30 centimeters.  Six cubic stones make a length of
  1-ken ( 180 centimeters ).  So, the stone is called ``kenchi-ishi,'' 
  which means ``know, `ken' ( unit ), stone.'' )
    
Later, engineer Eiji Hiraga worked on the river course renovation of 
the Tama River.  The cylinder shape water divide for the Nikaryou Canal 
( Nikaryou-entou-bunsui ) is one of his works.  It is located in 
Kawasaki City, Kanagawa Prefecture.  ( Nikaryou-entou-bunsui --> 
http://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/
%E4%B9%85%E5%9C%B0%E5%86%86%E7%AD%92%E5%88%86%E6%B0%B4 ) 

Eiji Hiraga's teacher was Eizaburou Ueno, who was a professor at 
Toukyou Imperial University ( now the University of Toukyou ).  
Eizaburou Ueno is known as the owner of the loyal dog ``Hachikou.''  
The dog is famous for its statue in front of Shibuya Station in 
Toukyou. 

The dog ``Hachikou'' was an Akita dog ( Akita-ken ).  Akita dogs are 
bigger than people today imagine.  They were bred from hunting dogs in 
Akita Prefecture.  The hunters were called ``matagui'' ( by T. Nihei 
2018 ). 
 
As for irrigation, water is supplied from rivers in snowy regions. In 
warm regions, Water is supplied from reservoirs.  As for sleigh, horse-
drawn sleigh transported lumbers in winter in Hokkaidou.  In summer, 
horse-drawn carriages were damaged from bad roads. 

The technique of making carriage was imported from the USA.  The 
technique for making sleigh was imported from Russia.  ( by Asahikawa 
City 2006.  ``New Asahikawa City history ( Shin-Asahikawa-shi-
shi ).'' )

( For sleigh, see Assabu Town in Hiyama sub-prefecture. )
 
#6

As for power generation, Naie Thermal Power Plant is located along the 
Ishikari River.  Chimneys with red and white stripes stand there.
Hokkaidou Electric Power runs the thermal power plant.  

From the Hokkaidou Expressway ( Dou-ou-dou ), chimneys of Sunagawa 
Power Plant are eye-catching.  Sunagawa and Naie are neighboring 
municipalities.  Both thermal power plants are located inland.  ( In 
Japan, almost all thermal power plants are located along the coast. )

( For thermal power plant, see Sunagawa City in Sorachi sub-
  prefecture and Atsuma Town in Iburi sub-prefecture. )
